 † † footnotetext: Mathematics Subject Classification. 2010 Primary 32G05; Secondary 58A14, 53C55, 14J32. 
 Locally extensions of ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -closed forms on compact generalized Hermitian manifolds 
 
 
 Kang Wei
 

 Abstract. 
 
 In this paper, we first get a criterion formula for whether a differential form is holomorphic with respect to the generalized complex structure induced by ϵ \epsilon . Next, we get the local extensions of ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -closed forms on a smooth family of compact generalized Hermitian manifolds by using this criterion. Finally, as an application, we use this extension to get the invariance of the generalized Hodge number of the deformations of compact
generalized Hermitian manifolds with ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma holds.

 Keywords: Deformations of complex structures, Hodge theory,
Hermitian and Kählerian manifolds. 
 
 
 
 1. Introduction 

 
 The generalized complex geometry was introduced by N. Hitchin and developed by M. Gualtieri, G. R. Cavalcanti and many others in [ 11 , 8 , 9 ] . It is a generalization of both complex geometry and symplectic geometry.
This new geometry provides an indeed broad platform for the people working in both mathematics and physics.
The concept of H H -twisted was introduced by P. S ˇ \check{S} evera and A. Weinstein in [ 24 ] .
The theory of deformations of complex structures can be dated back to Riemann, and extensively
studied by K. Kodaira, D. C. Spencer, N. Nirenberg, M. Kuranishi and many other great mathematicians in [ 12 , 20 , 13 ] .
The deformation theory of generalized complex geometry is first studied by M. Gualtieri, R. Goto, Yi Li and so on by using Kodaira-Spencer-Kuranishi’s method in [ 8 , 7 , 14 ] . 
 
 
 In this paper, we extend the results of local extensions of ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -closed forms on a smooth family of compact Hermitian manifolds in [ 22 ] to those in generalized case.
To be precise, if X X is a compact generalized complex manifold with generalized complex structure J J , we have the + i +i -eigenvalue subspace L L in T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} with respect to the generalized complex structure J J . We denote L ∗ L^{\ast} as its duality space with respect to the pair which is defined in Definition 2.1 below and we can identify L ∗ L^{\ast} with L ¯ \overline{L} since < L , L > = 0 <L,L>=0 .
We also know that ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} has a Clifford’s decomposition 
 

 
 
 ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \displaystyle\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} 
 = \displaystyle= 
 U − n ​ ( X ) ⊕ U − n + 1 ​ ( X ) ⊕ … ⊕ U n ​ ( X ) , \displaystyle U^{-n}(X)\oplus U^{-n+1}(X)\oplus...\oplus U^{n}(X), 
 
 
 where 
 

 
 
 U − n ​ ( X ) \displaystyle U^{-n}(X) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 { ρ ∈ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ ∣ L ⋅ ρ = 0 } , \displaystyle\{\rho\in\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast}\mid L\cdot\rho=0\}, 
 
 
 
 U k ​ ( X ) \displaystyle U^{k}(X) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 ∧ k + n L ∗ ⋅ U − n ( X ) ( n = d i m ℂ X ) , \displaystyle\wedge^{k+n}L^{\ast}\cdot U^{-n}(X)~~(n=dim_{\mathbb{C}}X), 
 
 
 which is defined in Definition 2.5 below. 
 
 
 Let π : 𝒳 → Δ ⊂ ℂ 1 \pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta\subset\mathbb{C}^{1} be a smooth family of generalized complex manifolds where π − 1 ​ ( 0 ) := X 0 = X \pi^{-1}(0):=X_{0}=X ,
 t t be the local coordinate in the unit disc Δ \Delta ,
 X ϵ X_{\epsilon} be compact generalized Hermitian manifold with generalized complex structure J ϵ J_{\epsilon} induced by the generalized Beltrami differentials ϵ := ϵ ⁡ ( t ) \epsilon:=\epsilon(t) .
We first introduce an isomorphism:

 where

 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 1 k ! σ i 1 ⋯ i k ( 1 + ϵ ∗ ) ( l i 1 ) ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ ( 1 + ϵ ∗ ) ( l i k ) ⋅ ( e ϵ ⋅ ρ 0 ) , \displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\sigma_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(1+\epsilon^{\ast})(l^{i_{1}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(1+\epsilon^{\ast})(l^{i_{k}})\cdot(e^{\epsilon}\cdot\rho_{0}), 
 
 
 
 e ϵ ​ ρ 0 \displaystyle e^{\epsilon}\rho_{0}

 ∑ i ≥ 0 1 i ! ​ ϵ i ⋅ ρ 0 . \displaystyle\sum_{i\geq 0}\frac{1}{i!}\epsilon^{i}\cdot\rho_{0}. 
 
 
 
 
 Then we get a criterion formula for whether a differential form is holomorphic with respect to the generalized complex structure J ϵ J_{\epsilon} induced by ϵ \epsilon . That is, 
 
 
 Theorem 1.1 . 
 
 (=Theorem 3.7 )
Assume that ‖ ϵ ‖ L ∞ < 1 ||\epsilon||_{L^{\infty}}<1 . For any σ ∈ U k − n ( X ) ⊂ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ ( 0 ≤ k ≤ 2 n ) , \sigma\in U^{k-n}(X)\subset\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast}(0\leq k\leq 2n), 
 

 
 
 ∂ ¯ t ( ℰ ( σ ) ) = 0 ⇔ ( [ ∂ , ϵ ⋅ ] + ∂ ¯ ) ∘ ( 1 − ϵ ϵ ∗ ) ( σ ) = 0 , \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{t}(\mathcal{E}(\sigma))=0\Leftrightarrow([\partial,\epsilon\cdot]+\overline{\partial})\circ(1-\epsilon\epsilon^{\ast})(\sigma)=0, 
 
 
 where ∂ ¯ t \overline{\partial}_{t} is the ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -operator on X ϵ := X ϵ ⁡ ( t ) X_{\epsilon}:=X_{\epsilon(t)} . 
 
 
 
 Next, under the assumption that X X satisfies some kind ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma, we get the following local extensions of ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -closed forms on a smooth family π : 𝒳 → Δ ⊂ ℂ 1 \pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta\subset\mathbb{C}^{1} of compact generalized Hermitian manifolds by using this criterion. The method we used here is parallel to that in [ 22 ] which originally came from [ 18 ] and developed in [ 28 , 27 , 5 , 15 , 25 , 26 , 31 , 32 , 21 , 17 ] . 
 
 
 Theorem 1.2 . 
 
 (=Theorem 4.1 )
Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact Hermitian generalized complex manifold. Assume that X ∈ 𝔹 k − 1 ∩ 𝕊 k + 1 . X\in\mathbb{B}^{k-1}\cap\mathbb{S}^{k+1}. Then for any σ 00 ∈ H ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) ​ ( − n ≤ k ≤ n ) \sigma_{00}\in H_{\overline{\partial}}^{k}(X)~(-n\leq k\leq n) , we can choose 
 
 
 

 
 
 σ t = σ 00 + ∑ i , j ≥ 1 t i ​ t ¯ j ​ σ i ​ j ∈ U k ​ ( X ) , \displaystyle\sigma_{t}=\sigma_{00}+\sum_{i,j\geq 1}t^{i}\bar{t}^{j}\sigma_{ij}\in U^{k}(X), 
 
 
 such that ℰ ⁡ ( σ t ) ∈ U k ​ ( X ϵ ) \mathcal{E}(\sigma_{t})\in U^{k}(X_{\epsilon}) and ∂ ¯ t ∘ ℰ ⁡ ( σ t ) = 0 \overline{\partial}_{t}\circ\mathcal{E}(\sigma_{t})=0 with | t | |t| small. 
 
 
 
 Finally, as an application, we use the above local extension formula to get the invariance of the generalized Hodge number of the deformations of compact
generalized Hermitian manifolds with ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma holds. That is, 
 
 
 Corollary 1.3 . 
 
 (=Corollary 5.3 )
Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold which satisfies ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma, then h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) h_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) is independent of t t , where − n ≤ k ≤ n -n\leq k\leq n , X t X_{t} is the generalized Hermitian manifold with generalized complex structure J ϵ J_{\epsilon} induced by ϵ := ϵ ⁡ ( t ) \epsilon:=\epsilon(t) .

 2. Preliminaries on compact generalized Hermitian manifolds 

 
 In this section, we review some basic definitions of compact H H -twisted generalized Hermitian manifolds. We refer the reader to [ 11 , 8 , 9 , 4 , 10 ] for details. We also give some propositions which will be used in this paper. 
 
 
 2.1. Basic knowledges 

 
 We first define a pair on smooth manifold X X which is a generalization of the pair < ∂ ∂ z i , d ​ z j > = δ i j <\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{i}},dz^{j}>=\delta^{j}_{i} . 
 
 
 Definition 2.1 . 
 
 Let M 2 ​ n M^{2n} be a smooth manifold with d ​ i ​ m ℝ ​ X = 2 ​ n dim_{\mathbb{R}}X=2n . We define the following pair on T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} : 
 
 
 

 
 
 < a , b > := ( ξ ⁡ ( Y ) + η ⁡ ( X ) ) , \displaystyle<a,b>:=(\xi(Y)+\eta(X)), 
 
 
 where a := X + ξ , b := Y + η ∈ T X ⊕ T X ∗ , X , Y ∈ T X , ξ , η ∈ T X ∗ a:=X+\xi,b:=Y+\eta\in T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast},~X,Y\in T_{X},~\xi,\eta\in T_{X}^{\ast} . 
 
 
 
 Remark 2.2 . 
 
 Comparing with the definition on Page 5 in [ 8 ] , we omit the coefficient 1 2 \frac{1}{2} for convenience.
And since < a , b > = < b , a > <a,b>=<b,a> , we also denote < a , b > <a,b> by a ⁡ ( b ) a(b) . 
 
 
 
 Next, we introduce the generalized complex structures on X X . 
 
 
 Definition 2.3 . 
 
 Let X 2 ​ n X^{2n} be a smooth manifold with d ​ i ​ m ℝ ​ X = 2 ​ n dim_{\mathbb{R}}X=2n . If there
exists an endomorphism J J on T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} which satisfies 
 
 
 

 
 
 J 2 = − 1 , J^{2}=-1, 
 
 
 and 
 

 
 
 < a , b > = < J ​ a , J ​ b > , where ​ a , b ∈ T X ⊕ T X ∗ , <a,b>=<Ja,Jb>,~~\mbox{where}~~a,b\in T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast}, 
 
 
 
 
 J J is called a generalized almost complex structure on X X . 
 
 
 
 Since J 2 = − 1 J^{2}=-1 , we may decompose T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} into the ± i \pm i -eigenvalue subspaces of J J : 
 

 
 
 T X ⊕ T X ∗ = L + L ¯ , T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast}=L+\overline{L}, 
 
 
 
 
 where L L be the + i +i -eigenvalue subspace of T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} .
We define H H -twisted Courant bracket for some H ∈ H 3 ​ ( M , ℝ ) H\in H^{3}(M,\mathbb{R}) as follow:
Let a = X + ξ , b = Y + η ∈ T X ⊕ T X ∗ a=X+\xi,b=Y+\eta\in T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} , where X , Y ∈ T X , ξ , η ∈ T X ∗ X,Y\in T_{X},~\xi,\eta\in T_{X}^{\ast} . 
 
 
 

 
 
 [ a , b ] H \displaystyle[a,b]_{H} 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 [ X + ξ , Y + η ] H \displaystyle[X+\xi,Y+\eta]_{H} 
 
 
 
 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 [ X , Y ] + L X ​ η − L Y ​ ξ − 1 2 ​ d ​ ( i X ​ η − i Y ​ ξ ) + i Y ​ i X ​ H , \displaystyle[X,Y]+L_{X}\eta-L_{Y}\xi-\frac{1}{2}d(i_{X}\eta-i_{Y}\xi)+i_{Y}i_{X}H, 
 
 
 where
 [ X , Y ] := X Y − Y X , L X η := d ∘ i X η + i X ∘ d η , i X [X,Y]:=XY-YX,L_{X}\eta:=d\circ i_{X}\eta+i_{X}\circ d\eta,~i_{X} is the contraction by the vector field X X .
If J J is called a generalized almost complex structure on X X and [ L , L ] H ⊂ L , [L,L]_{H}\subset L, we say J J is integrable and call it a generalized complex structure on X X . 
 
 
 We now introduce Clifford actions on ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} . 
 
 
 Definition 2.4 . 
 
 Let σ ∈ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \sigma\in\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} and a := X + ξ ∈ T X ⊕ T X ∗ a:=X+\xi\in T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} , where X ∈ T X , ξ ∈ T X ∗ X\in T_{X},~\xi\in T_{X}^{\ast} ,
we define Clifford actions on ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} : 
 

 
 
 a ⋅ σ := ( X + ξ ) ⋅ σ := i X ​ σ + ξ ∧ σ . a\cdot\sigma:=(X+\xi)\cdot\sigma:=i_{X}\sigma+\xi\wedge\sigma. 
 
 
 
 
 By direct computation, we know that 
 

 
 (2.1) 
 
 a ⋅ b ⋅ σ + b ⋅ a ⋅ σ \displaystyle a\cdot b\cdot\sigma+b\cdot a\cdot\sigma 
 = \displaystyle= 
 a ⁡ ( b ) ​ σ , \displaystyle a(b)\sigma, 
 
 
 where a , b ∈ T X ⊕ T X ∗ a,b\in T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} . 
 
 
 
 If J J is integrable, we have that L ¯ ​ ( L ¯ ) = 0 \overline{L}(\overline{L})=0 and thus 
 

 
 
 a ⋅ b ⋅ σ + b ⋅ a ⋅ σ \displaystyle a\cdot b\cdot\sigma+b\cdot a\cdot\sigma 
 = \displaystyle= 
 0 . \displaystyle 0. 
 
 
 Then we get an exterior algebra which denote as ∧ ∙ L ¯ \wedge^{\bullet}\overline{L} . More generally, we have that 
 
 
 

 
 
 a ⋅ b ⋅ σ \displaystyle a\cdot b\cdot\sigma 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ( − 1 ) p ​ q ​ b ⋅ a ⋅ σ , \displaystyle(-1)^{pq}b\cdot a\cdot\sigma, 
 
 
 where a ∈ ∧ p L ¯ , b ∈ ∧ q L ¯ . a\in\wedge^{p}\overline{L},~b\in\wedge^{q}\overline{L}. 
Moreover, since < L ; L > = 0 <L;L>=0 , we can identify L ¯ \overline{L} with the duality space L ∗ L^{\ast} of L L with respect to the pair < ⋅ , ⋅ > <\cdot,\cdot> in Definition 2.1 .
And Courant bracket can be extended into a Schouten bracket on ∧ ∙ L ∗ = ∧ ∙ L ¯ \wedge^{\bullet}L^{\ast}=\wedge^{\bullet}\overline{L} which we still denote by
 [ ⋅ , ⋅ ] H [\cdot,\cdot]_{H} as follows: 
 
 
 

 
 
 [ a , b ] H := ∑ i , j [ a i , b j ] H ⋅ a 1 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ a i ^ ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ a p ⋅ b 1 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ b j ^ ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ b q , \displaystyle[a,b]_{H}:=\sum_{i,j}[a_{i},b_{j}]_{H}\cdot a_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot\hat{a_{i}}\cdot\cdots\cdot a_{p}\cdot b_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot\hat{b_{j}}\cdot\cdots\cdot b_{q}, 
 
 
 where a = a 1 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ a p ∈ ∧ p L ∗ , b = b 1 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ b q ∈ ∧ q L ∗ , a i ^ a=a_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot a_{p}\in\wedge^{p}L^{\ast},~b=b_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot b_{q}\in\wedge^{q}L^{\ast},~\hat{a_{i}} means omit a i a_{i} . 
 
 
 Using the Clifford actions, we now introduce the following Clifford’s decomposition of ∧ ∙ T M ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{M}^{\ast} . 
 
 
 Definition 2.5 . 
 
 ( [ 4 ] , Page 13)
Let ( M , J ) (M,J) be a generalized complex manifold where J J is its generalized complex structure. Then we can define 
 
 
 

 
 
 U − n ​ ( X ) \displaystyle U^{-n}(X) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 { ρ ∈ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ ∣ L ⋅ ρ = 0 } , \displaystyle\{\rho\in\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast}\mid L\cdot\rho=0\}, 
 
 
 
 U k ​ ( X ) \displaystyle U^{k}(X) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 ∧ k + n L ∗ ⋅ U − n ( X ) . \displaystyle\wedge^{k+n}L^{\ast}\cdot U^{-n}(X). 
 
 
 
 
 
 And we know that U k ​ ( X ) U^{k}(X) is the ( − k ) ​ i (-k)i -eigenvalue subspace of J J and U − n ​ ( X ) U^{-n}(X) is a line bundle which we call it the canonical bundle of J J .
Thus we can get the Clifford’s decomposition of ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} : 
 

 
 
 ∧ ∙ T X ∗ = U − n ( X ) ⊕ U − n + 1 ( X ) ⊕ … ⊕ U n ( X ) , \displaystyle\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast}=U^{-n}(X)\oplus U^{-n+1}(X)\oplus...\oplus U^{n}(X), 
 
 
 where n = d ​ i ​ m ℂ ​ X n=dim_{\mathbb{C}}X . 
 
 
 We now introduce the twisted de Rham differential d H d_{H} for some H ∈ H 3 ​ ( X , ℝ ) H\in H^{3}(X,\mathbb{R}) on ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} as follows: 
 
 
 Definition 2.6 . 
 
 

 
 
 d H : ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \displaystyle d_{H}:\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 ∧ ∙ T X ∗ , \displaystyle\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast}, 
 
 
 
 σ \displaystyle\sigma 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto 
 d ​ σ − H ∧ σ . \displaystyle d\sigma-H\wedge\sigma. 
 
 
 
 
 We also introduce the twisted Dolbeault operator ∂ H \partial_{H} and ∂ ¯ H \overline{\partial}_{H} by 
 

 
 
 ∂ H := π k − 1 ∘ d H : U k ( X ) \displaystyle\partial_{H}:=\pi_{k-1}\circ d_{H}:U^{k}(X) 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 U k − 1 ​ ( X ) , \displaystyle U^{k-1}(X), 
 
 
 
 ∂ ¯ H := π k + 1 ∘ d H : U k ​ ( X ) \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{H}:=\pi_{k+1}\circ d_{H}:U^{k}(X) 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 U k + 1 ​ ( X ) , \displaystyle U^{k+1}(X), 
 
 
 where π k \pi_{k} is the projection onto U k ​ ( X ) U^{k}(X) . 
 
 
 
 We know that J J is integrable if and only
if  d H = ∂ H + ∂ ¯ H d_{H}=\partial_{H}+\overline{\partial}_{H} (Page 51 in [ 8 ] ). 
 
 
 We now introduce the complex ( ∧ ∙ L ∗ , d L ) (\wedge^{\bullet}L^{\ast},d_{L}) and show its relationship with the complex ( ∧ ∙ T X ∗ , ∂ ¯ H ) (\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast},\overline{\partial}_{H}) . 
 
 
 Definition 2.7 . 
 
 We define the Lie derivation d L d_{L} as follows: 
 

 
 
 d L : ∧ k L ∗ \displaystyle d_{L}:\wedge^{k}L^{\ast} 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 ∧ k + 1 L ∗ , \displaystyle\wedge^{k+1}L^{\ast}, 
 
 
 
 a \displaystyle a 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto 
 d L ​ a \displaystyle d_{L}a 
 
 
 
 
 where 
 

 
 
 d L ​ a ​ ( x 0 , … ​ x k ) \displaystyle d_{L}a(x_{0},...x_{k}) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 ∑ i ( − 1 ) i ​ p ​ ( x i ) ​ a ​ ( x 0 , … , x i ^ , … , x k ) \displaystyle\sum_{i}(-1)^{i}p(x_{i})a(x_{0},...,\hat{x_{i}},...,x_{k}) 
 
 
 
 
 
 + ∑ i < j ( − 1 ) i + j a ( [ x i , x j ] H , x 0 , … , x i ^ , … , x j ^ , … , x k ) , \displaystyle+\sum_{i<j}(-1)^{i+j}a([x_{i},x_{j}]_{H},x_{0},...,\hat{x_{i}},...,\hat{x_{j}},...,x_{k}), 
 
 
 where a ∈ ∧ k L ∗ , x i ∈ L ( 0 ≤ i ≤ k ) , p : L → T X a\in\wedge^{k}L^{\ast},x_{i}\in L(0\leq i\leq k),p:L\to T_{X} is the projection which is called the anchor.
And we have the following relationship: 
 

 
 
 ∂ ¯ H ​ ( a ⋅ ρ ) = d L ​ ( a ) ⋅ ρ + ( − 1 ) k ​ a ⋅ ∂ ¯ H ​ ρ , \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{H}(a\cdot\rho)=d_{L}(a)\cdot\rho+(-1)^{k}a\cdot\overline{\partial}_{H}\rho, 
 
 
 where a ∈ ∧ k L ∗ , ρ ∈ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ , a\in\wedge^{k}L^{\ast},\rho\in\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast}, 
 
 
 
 Remark 2.8 . 
 
 We now give a discussion about the order of operators d H , ∂ H , ∂ ¯ H d_{H},\partial_{H},\overline{\partial}_{H} .
Since d H ​ σ := d ​ σ − H ∧ σ d_{H}\sigma:=d\sigma-H\wedge\sigma , we know that d H d_{H} is an operator of order 1. By the definition of d L d_{L} above, we know that
 d L d_{L} is an operator of order 1. For any α := a ⋅ ρ ∈ U k ​ ( X ) , \alpha:=a\cdot\rho\in U^{k}(X), where a ∈ ∧ k + n L ∗ , ρ ∈ U − n ( X ) a\in\wedge^{k+n}L^{\ast},\rho\in U^{-n}(X) , we have that ∂ H ρ = 0 \partial_{H}\rho=0 and thus
 ∂ ¯ H ​ ( a ⋅ ρ ) = d L ​ ( a ) ⋅ ρ + ( − 1 ) k ​ a ⋅ ∂ ¯ H ​ ρ = d L ​ ( a ) ⋅ ρ + ( − 1 ) k ​ a ⋅ d H ​ ρ \overline{\partial}_{H}(a\cdot\rho)=d_{L}(a)\cdot\rho+(-1)^{k}a\cdot\overline{\partial}_{H}\rho=d_{L}(a)\cdot\rho+(-1)^{k}a\cdot d_{H}\rho . So ∂ ¯ H \overline{\partial}_{H} is an operator of order 1. ∂ H := d H − ∂ ¯ H \partial_{H}:=d_{H}-\overline{\partial}_{H} is also an operator of order 1. 
 
 
 
 From now on, we simply denote d H , ∂ H , ∂ ¯ H , [ ⋅ , ⋅ ] H d_{H},\partial_{H},\overline{\partial}_{H},[\cdot,\cdot]_{H} as d , ∂ , ∂ ¯ , [ ⋅ , ⋅ ] d,\partial,\overline{\partial},[\cdot,\cdot] respectively. 
 
 
 We list the following lemma which will be used later: 
 
 
 Lemma 2.9 (Lemma 2 in Page 8, [ 14 ] , or [ 16 ] ) . 
 
 For any a , b ∈ ∧ 2 L ∗ , σ ∈ ∧ ∙ T ∗ X a,b\in\wedge^{2}L^{\ast},\sigma\in\wedge^{\bullet}T^{\ast}_{X} , we have that 
 

 
 
 [ a , b ] ⋅ σ = a ⋅ d ⁡ ( b ⋅ σ ) + b ⋅ d ⁡ ( a ⋅ σ ) − a ⋅ b ⋅ d ​ σ − d ⁡ ( a ⋅ b ⋅ σ ) , [a,b]\cdot\sigma=a\cdot d(b\cdot\sigma)+b\cdot d(a\cdot\sigma)-a\cdot b\cdot d\sigma-d(a\cdot b\cdot\sigma), 
 
 
 where ⋅ \cdot is denoted as the Clifford action. 
 
 
 
 
 2.2. Some elliptic operators 

 
 We first introduce a generalized Hermitian metric on T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} . 
 
 
 Definition 2.10 . 
 
 ( [ 9 ] , Page 3)
Let ( X , J ) (X,J) be a generalized complex manifold and G G be an endomorphism on T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} with G 2 = 1 , G ​ J = J ​ G G^{2}=1,GJ=JG . We define 
 

 
 
 ( T X ⊕ T X ∗ ) ⊗ ( T X ⊕ T X ∗ ) \displaystyle(T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast})\otimes(T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast}) 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 C ∞ ​ ( X ) , \displaystyle C^{\infty}(X), 
 
 
 
 a , b \displaystyle a,b 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto 
 < G ​ a , b > \displaystyle<Ga,b> 
 
 
 
 
 where a , b ∈ T X ⊕ T X ∗ a,b\in T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} and < ⋅ , ⋅ > <\cdot,\cdot> is defined in Definition 2.1 . Since G 2 = 1 , G ​ J = J ​ G G^{2}=1,GJ=JG , < G ⋅ , ⋅ > <G\cdot,\cdot> is a positive-definite and Hermitian-symmetric metric on T X ⊕ T X ∗ T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast} . And we call ( X , G ) (X,G) a generalized Hermitian manifold. 
 
 
 
 The restriction of this metric < G ⋅ , ⋅ > <G\cdot,\cdot> to the the sub-bundle T X T_{X} can be written as a Hermitian metric g ~ := g − b ​ g − 1 ​ b \tilde{g}:=g-bg^{-1}b , where g g is a Hermitian metric and b b is a 2-form.
And the volume element induced by this metric is 
 
 
 

 
 
 d ​ V g ~ \displaystyle dV_{\tilde{g}} 
 = \displaystyle= 
 d ​ e ​ t ​ ( g − b ​ g − 1 ​ b ) d ​ e ​ t ​ ( g ) ​ d ​ V g \displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{det(g-bg^{-1}b)}}{\sqrt{det(g)}}dV_{g} 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 d ​ e ​ t ​ ( g + b ) d ​ e ​ t ​ ( g ) ​ d ​ V g . \displaystyle\frac{det(g+b)}{det(g)}dV_{g}. 
 
 
 
 
 Analogous to the ordinary complex case, we introduce the Hodge ∗ \ast -operator and use it to define an inner product on ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} which we call it Born-Infeld inner products.
Since G 2 = 1 , G^{2}=1, we have the decomposition T X ⊕ T X ∗ = C + ⊕ C − , T_{X}\oplus T_{X}^{\ast}=C_{+}\oplus C_{-}, where C ± C_{\pm} is ± 1 \pm 1 -eigenvalue subspace of G G respectively. And we choose an orthonormal real basis { a 1 , ⋯ , a 2 ​ n } \{a_{1},\cdots,a_{2n}\} of C + C_{+} with respect to the metric < G ⋅ , ⋅ > . <G\cdot,\cdot>. 
We define the Hodge- ∗ \ast operator by 
 

 
 
 ∗ \displaystyle\ast 
 = \displaystyle= 
 a 1 ⋅ a 2 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ a 2 ​ n , \displaystyle a_{1}\cdot a_{2}\cdot\cdots\cdot a_{2n}, 
 
 
 which is a product of an oriented orthonormal basis for C + C_{+} . We have already known that ∗ \ast is a real operator, that is, ∗ = ∗ ¯ \ast=\overline{\ast} .
We now introduce the Born-Infeld inner product ( ⋅ , ⋅ ) (\cdot,\cdot) on ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} : 
 

 
 (2.2) 
 
 ( α , β ) \displaystyle(\alpha,\beta) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 ∫ X α ∧ σ ⁡ ( ∗ ) ​ β ¯ , \displaystyle\int_{X}\alpha\wedge\sigma(\ast)\bar{\beta}, 
 
 
 
 ‖ α ‖ 2 \displaystyle||\alpha||^{2} 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:=

 where σ ( a 1 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ a 2 ​ n ) := a 2 ​ n ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ a 1 \sigma(a_{1}\cdot\cdots\cdot a_{2n}):=a_{2n}\cdot\cdots\cdot a_{1} . 
 
 
 We now introduce some elliptic operators analogous to the complex case.
Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold. Denote d ∗ , ∂ ∗ , ∂ ¯ ∗ d^{\ast},~\partial^{\ast},~\overline{\partial}^{\ast} as the adjoint operators of d , ∂ , ∂ ¯ d,~\partial,~\overline{\partial} with respect to the Born-Infeld inner product ( ⋅ , ⋅ ) (\cdot,\cdot) respectively, that is, for any α , β ∈ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \alpha,\beta\in\wedge^{\bullet}T_{X}^{\ast} ,

 = \displaystyle=

 = \displaystyle=

 Remark 2.11 . 
 
 Now, we give a discussion about the order of ∂ ∗ , ∂ ¯ ∗ \partial^{\ast},~\overline{\partial}^{\ast} .
We know that ∂ ∗ = ∗ ∂ ¯ ∗ − 1 \partial^{\ast}=\ast\overline{\partial}\ast^{-1} and ∂ ¯ ∗ = ∗ ∂ ∗ − 1 \overline{\partial}^{\ast}=\ast\partial\ast^{-1} .
Since ∂ , ∂ ¯ \partial,\overline{\partial} are operators of order 1, we have that ∂ ∗ , ∂ ¯ ∗ \partial^{\ast},\overline{\partial}^{\ast} are also operators of order 1 
 
 
 
 We also define the Laplacian operators by

 Then one can show that △ ∂ ¯ ~\triangle_{\overline{\partial}} are a self-adjoint operator with respect to inner product ( ⋅ , ⋅ ) (\cdot,\cdot) and

 ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ G B ​ C , \displaystyle(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}G_{BC}, 
 
 
 
 G A ( ∂ ¯ ∂ ) ∗ ( ∂ ¯ ∂ ) \displaystyle G_{A}(\overline{\partial}\partial)^{\ast}(\overline{\partial}\partial) 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ( ∂ ¯ ∂ ) ∗ ( ∂ ¯ ∂ ) G A , \displaystyle(\overline{\partial}\partial)^{\ast}(\overline{\partial}\partial)G_{A}, 
 
 
 
 G B ​ C ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) \displaystyle G_{BC}(\partial\overline{\partial}) 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ G A , \displaystyle(\partial\overline{\partial})G_{A}, 
 
 
 
 ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ G B ​ C \displaystyle(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}G_{BC} 
 = \displaystyle= 
 G A ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ . \displaystyle G_{A}(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 By definition of Δ B ​ C , Δ A \Delta_{BC},~\Delta_{A} , we can get the first four formula. For example, we have that 
 

 
 
 Δ B ​ C ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ \displaystyle\Delta_{BC}(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast} 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ = ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ Δ B ​ C . \displaystyle(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}=(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}\Delta_{BC}. 
 
 
 
 
 By the first formula, we have that 
 
 
 

 
 
 G B ​ C ​ Δ B ​ C ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ G B ​ C \displaystyle G_{BC}\Delta_{BC}(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}G_{BC}

 = ( 1 − ℍ B ​ C ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ G B ​ C \displaystyle=(1-\mathbb{H}_{BC})(\partial\overline{\partial})(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}G_{BC}

 = \displaystyle=

 So we prove the fifth formula and the proofs of the rest formulas are similar.

 2.3. Deformations 

 
 In this subsection, we introduce some propositions about deformations of compact generalized Hermitian manifolds.
Let π : 𝒳 → Δ ⊂ ℂ 1 \pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta\subset\mathbb{C}^{1} be a smooth family of generalized complex manifolds where π − 1 ​ ( 0 ) := X 0 := X \pi^{-1}(0):=X_{0}:=X is a compact generalized Hermitian manifold, t t be the local holomorphic coordinate on the unit disc Δ ⊂ ℂ 1 \Delta\subset\mathbb{C}^{1} , ϵ ⁡ ( t ) \epsilon(t) be a generalized Beltrami differentials from Kodaira-Spencer-Kuranishi’s theory, and M ϵ ⁡ ( t ) M_{\epsilon(t)} be the generalized
Kähler manifold with generalized complex structure induced by ϵ ⁡ ( t ) \epsilon(t) .
We have already known that 
 

 
 
 T X ⊕ T X ∗ = L ⊕ L ∗ , T_{X}\oplus T^{\ast}_{X}=L\oplus L^{\ast}, 
 
 
 where L L is the + i +i -eigenvalue subspace of the generalized complex structure J J on X X .
We assume that

 = \displaystyle=

 Proposition 2.21 . 
 
 

 
 
 ξ i = l q ​ ( ξ i ) ​ ( 1 + ϵ ) ​ ( l q ) , \displaystyle\xi_{i}=l^{q}(\xi_{i})(1+\epsilon)(l_{q}),

 2.4. ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -Lemma 

 
 In this subsection, we discuss some propositions about ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma on X X . 
 
 
 Definition 2.23 ( ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -Lemma) . 
 
 We say ( X , J ) (X,J) satisfies the ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -Lemma if 
 

 
 
 I ​ m ​ ( ∂ ) ∩ K ​ e ​ r ​ ( ∂ ¯ ) = I ​ m ​ ( ∂ ¯ ) ∩ K ​ e ​ r ​ ( ∂ ) = I ​ m ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) . Im(\partial)\cap Ker(\overline{\partial})=Im(\overline{\partial})\cap Ker(\partial)=Im(\partial\overline{\partial}). 
 
 
 
 
 
 Now we give some weaker definitions: 
 
 
 Definition 2.24 . 
 
 If for any ∂ φ ∈ U k ​ ( X ) \partial\varphi\in U^{k}(X) with ∂ ¯ ​ ∂ φ = 0 \overline{\partial}\partial\varphi=0 , the equation 
 

 
 
 ∂ ¯ ​ σ = ∂ φ \overline{\partial}\sigma=\partial\varphi 
 
 
 has a solution σ ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X ) \sigma\in U^{k-1}(X)  (a ∂ \partial -exact solution σ := ∂ σ 1 ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X ) \sigma:=\partial\sigma_{1}\in U^{k-1}(X) ), we denote as X ∈ 𝕊 k X\in\mathbb{S}^{k} 
 ( X ∈ 𝔹 k X\in\mathbb{B}^{k} );
similarly, if for any φ ∈ U k + 1 ​ ( X ) \varphi\in U^{k+1}(X) with ∂ ¯ ​ φ = 0 \overline{\partial}\varphi=0 , the equation 
 

 
 
 ∂ ¯ ​ σ = ∂ φ \overline{\partial}\sigma=\partial\varphi 
 
 
 has a solution σ ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X ) \sigma\in U^{k-1}(X)  (a ∂ \partial -exact solution σ := ∂ σ 1 ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X ) \sigma:=\partial\sigma_{1}\in U^{k-1}(X) ), we denote as X ∈ 𝒮 k X\in\mathcal{S}^{k} 
 ( X ∈ ℬ k X\in\mathcal{B}^{k} ). 
 
 
 
 Thus, we have the following relationship between the two definitions above: 
 
 
 Lemma 2.25 . 
 
 If X X satisfies ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma, we have that X ∈ 𝔹 k X\in\mathbb{B}^{k} for any − n ≤ k ≤ n -n\leq k\leq n . 
 
 
 
 Now , we give the following lemmas which will be used in the paper later. 
 
 
 Proposition 2.26 . 
 
 Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold. If the ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -equation 
 
 
 

 
 (2.26) 
 
 ∂ ∂ ¯ ​ x = y \displaystyle\partial\overline{\partial}x=y 
 
 
 
 
 has a solution, then
 x = ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ G B ​ C ​ y x=(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}G_{BC}y is also a solution which has the minimum L 2 L^{2} -norm with respect to the Born-Infeld inner product definite by Formula 2.2 .

 = \displaystyle=

 = \displaystyle=

 Then

 = \displaystyle=

 G A ​ ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ ​ y \displaystyle G_{A}(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}y 
 = \displaystyle=

 Thus 
 
 
 

 
 
 x 3 \displaystyle x_{3} 
 = \displaystyle=

 ‖ x ‖ 2 \displaystyle||x||^{2}

 Proposition 2.27 . 
 
 Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold and assume that X ∈ 𝔹 k − 1 X\in\mathbb{B}^{k-1} . For any [ σ ] ∈ H ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) [\sigma]\in H_{\overline{\partial}}^{k}(X) . Choose a representation of [ σ ] [\sigma] and
we still denote it as σ ∈ H ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) \sigma\in H_{\overline{\partial}}^{k}(X) .
Then there exists some β σ ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X ) \beta_{\sigma}\in U^{k-1}(X) , such that γ σ := σ + ∂ ¯ ​ β σ \gamma_{\sigma}:=\sigma+\overline{\partial}\beta_{\sigma} is also a representation of [ σ ] [\sigma] with d ​ γ σ = 0 d\gamma_{\sigma}=0 . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 If γ σ \gamma_{\sigma} exists, we can get that there exists some β σ ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X ) \beta_{\sigma}\in U^{k-1}(X) such that 
 

 
 
 γ σ = σ + ∂ ¯ ​ β σ . \gamma_{\sigma}=\sigma+\overline{\partial}\beta_{\sigma}. 
 
 
 Thus, 
 

 
 
 0 \displaystyle 0 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ γ σ ​ ( d ​ γ σ = 0 ) \displaystyle\partial\gamma_{\sigma}~(d\gamma_{\sigma}=0) 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ σ + ∂ ∂ ¯ ​ β σ . \displaystyle\partial\sigma+\partial\overline{\partial}\beta_{\sigma}. 
 
 
 That is 
 

 
 
 ∂ σ = − ∂ ∂ ¯ β σ . \displaystyle\partial\sigma=-\partial\overline{\partial}\beta_{\sigma}. 
 
 
 So by the assumption that X ∈ 𝔹 k − 1 X\in\mathbb{B}^{k-1} and Proposition 2.26 above, we can get a solution β σ = − ( ∂ ∂ ¯ ) ∗ G B ​ C ∂ σ ∈ U k − 1 ( X ) \beta_{\sigma}=-(\partial\overline{\partial})^{\ast}G_{BC}\partial\sigma\in U^{k-1}(X) 
and γ σ = σ + ∂ ¯ ​ β σ \gamma_{\sigma}=\sigma+\overline{\partial}\beta_{\sigma} is desired.
∎ 
 
 
 
 
 
 3. Criterion for holomorphism 

 
 Let π : 𝒳 → Δ ⊂ ℂ 1 \pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta\subset\mathbb{C}^{1} be a smooth family of generalized complex manifolds where the centre manifold π − 1 ​ ( 0 ) := X 0 := X \pi^{-1}(0):=X_{0}:=X is a compact generalized Hermitian manifold.
In this section , we get a criterion formula for whether a differential form is holomorphic with respect to the new generalized complex structure ( X ϵ , J ϵ ) (X_{\epsilon},J_{\epsilon}) induced by ϵ \epsilon . The method we use is parallel to
that in [ 22 ] .
We first define a map between ∧ k L ∗ ⋅ U − n ( X ) \wedge^{k}L^{\ast}\cdot U^{-n}(X) and ∧ k L ϵ ∗ ⋅ U − n ( X ϵ ) \wedge^{k}L^{\ast}_{\epsilon}\cdot U^{-n}(X_{\epsilon}) . 
 
 
 Definition 3.1 . 
 
 

 
 
 ℰ : ∧ k L ∗ ⋅ U − n ( X ) \displaystyle\mathcal{E}:\wedge^{k}L^{\ast}\cdot U^{-n}(X) 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 ∧ k L ϵ ∗ ⋅ U − n ( X ϵ ) , \displaystyle\wedge^{k}L^{\ast}_{\epsilon}\cdot U^{-n}(X_{\epsilon}), 
 
 
 
 σ \displaystyle\sigma 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto 
 ℰ ⁡ ( σ ) \displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\sigma) 
 
 
 where 
 

 
 
 σ \displaystyle\sigma 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 1 k ! σ i 1 ⋯ i k l i 1 ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ l i k ⋅ ρ 0 , l i α ∈ L ∗ , ρ 0 ∈ U − n ( X ) , \displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\sigma_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}l^{i_{1}}\cdot\cdots\cdot l^{i_{k}}\cdot\rho_{0},~~l^{i_{\alpha}}\in L^{\ast},\rho_{0}\in U^{-n}(X), 
 
 
 
 ℰ ⁡ ( σ ) \displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\sigma) 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 1 k ! σ i 1 ⋯ i k ( 1 + ϵ ∗ ) ( l i 1 ) ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ ( 1 + ϵ ∗ ) ( l i k ) ⋅ ( e ϵ ⋅ ρ 0 ) , \displaystyle\frac{1}{k!}\sigma_{i_{1}\cdots i_{k}}(1+\epsilon^{\ast})(l^{i_{1}})\cdot\cdots\cdot(1+\epsilon^{\ast})(l^{i_{k}})\cdot(e^{\epsilon}\cdot\rho_{0}), 
 
 
 
 e ϵ ⋅ ρ 0 \displaystyle e^{\epsilon}\cdot\rho_{0} 
 : ⁣ = \displaystyle:= 
 ∑ i ≥ 0 1 i ! ​ ϵ i ⋅ ρ 0 , ϵ i := ϵ ⋅ ϵ ⋅ ⋯ ⋅ ϵ ⋅ ⏞ i . \displaystyle\sum_{i\geq 0}\frac{1}{i!}\epsilon^{i}\cdot\rho_{0},~\epsilon^{i}:=\overbrace{\epsilon\cdot\epsilon\cdot\cdots\cdot\epsilon\cdot}^{i}. 
 
 
 
 
 
 About e ϵ e^{\epsilon} and ℰ \mathcal{E} , we have the following two propositions. 
 
 
 Proposition 3.2 . 
 
 

 
 
 e ϵ ⋅ : ( L ⊕ L ∗ ) ⋅ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \displaystyle e^{\epsilon}\cdot:(L\oplus L^{\ast})\cdot\wedge^{\bullet}T^{\ast}_{X} 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 ∧ ∙ T ∗ X , \displaystyle\wedge^{\bullet}T^{\ast}_{X}, 
 
 
 
 l ⋅ ρ \displaystyle l\cdot\rho 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto

 e ϵ ∗ ⋅ : ( L ⊕ L ∗ ) ⋅ ∧ ∙ T X ∗ \displaystyle e^{\epsilon^{\ast}}\cdot:(L\oplus L^{\ast})\cdot\wedge^{\bullet}T^{\ast}_{X} 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 ∧ ∙ T ∗ X . \displaystyle\wedge^{\bullet}T^{\ast}_{X}. 
 
 
 
 l ⋅ ρ \displaystyle l\cdot\rho 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto

 Proof.

 Now, we get the criterion formula for whether a differential form is holomorphic with respect to the generalized complex structure J ϵ J_{\epsilon} induced by ϵ \epsilon .

 4. Locally extensions 

 
 In this section , we get the following local extensions of ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -closed forms on a smooth family π : 𝒳 → Δ ⊂ ℂ 1 \pi:\mathcal{X}\to\Delta\subset\mathbb{C}^{1} of compact generalized Hermitian manifolds. The method we used is parallel to that in [ 22 ] which originally came from [ 28 , 27 , 5 , 15 , 25 , 26 , 31 , 32 , 21 , 17 ] . This is also an extension of local extensions of canonical forms in [ 30 ] .

 − ∑ i + j = p , k + l = q , i + k ≥ 1 ϵ i ​ k ⋅ ∂ ( ∑ r + m = j , s + n = l , r + s ≥ 1 , m + n ≥ 1 ϵ r ​ s ⋅ ∂ σ ~ m ​ n ) \displaystyle-\sum_{i+j=p,k+l=q,i+k\geq 1}\epsilon_{ik}\cdot\partial(\sum_{r+m=j,s+n=l,r+s\geq 1,m+n\geq 1}\epsilon_{rs}\cdot\partial\tilde{\sigma}_{mn}) 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 0 . \displaystyle 0. 
 
 
 
 
 The third equality holds since the integrable condition; the fourth equality holds since Lemma 2.9 and the induction. 
 
 
 Then by the assumption H ∂ ¯ k + 1 ​ ( X ) = 0 , H^{k+1}_{\overline{\partial}}(X)=0, we have that τ ~ p ​ q ∈ I ​ m ​ ∂ ¯ , \tilde{\tau}_{pq}\in Im\overline{\partial}, that is, 
 

 
 
 τ ~ p ​ q = ∂ ¯ ​ σ ~ p ​ q \displaystyle\tilde{\tau}_{pq}=\overline{\partial}\tilde{\sigma}_{pq} 
 
 
 has a solution σ ~ p ​ q \tilde{\sigma}_{pq} . 
 
 
 We get the regularity of σ ~ t \tilde{\sigma}_{t} by using the same elliptic estimates as that in the above theorem. 
 
 
 ∎ 
 
 
 
 
 5. Applications 

 
 In this section, we use the extension formula in Theorem 4.1 to get the invariance of the generalized Hodge number of the deformations of compact
generalized Hermitian manifolds with ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma holds. The method we used here is parallel to that in [ 12 , 22 ] .
Our idea is as follows: Since G ∂ ¯ G_{\overline{\partial}} is strongly elliptic, by Theorem 7.3 on Page 326 in [ 12 ] , we have that h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) := d ​ i ​ m ℂ ​ H ∂ ¯ t k − 1 ​ ( X t ) h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}):=dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{k-1}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) is upper-semicontinuous in t t , that is, 
 
 
 

 
 
 h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) \displaystyle h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) 
 ≤ \displaystyle\leq 
 h ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) ​ if ​ | t | ​ sufficient small . \displaystyle h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}}(X)~\mbox{if}~|t|~\mbox{sufficient small}. 
 
 
 
 
 In Theorem 4.1 , we have given a map from H ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) H_{\overline{\partial}}^{k}(X) to H ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) H_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) 
which is Map 5.1 below. If it is injective, we have that h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) ≥ h ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t})\geq h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}}(X) and thus get the result. 
 
 
 Proposition 5.1 . 
 
 Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold. If we assume that h ∂ ¯ t k − 1 ​ ( X t ) h^{k-1}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) is independent of t t , and X ∈ 𝔹 k − 1 ∩ 𝕊 k + 1 , X\in\mathbb{B}^{k-1}\cap\mathbb{S}^{k+1}, we have that 
 
 
 

 
 (5.1) 
 
 H ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) \displaystyle H_{\overline{\partial}}^{k}(X) 
 → \displaystyle\to 
 H ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) \displaystyle H_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) 
 
 
 
 σ 00 \displaystyle\sigma_{00} 
 ↦ \displaystyle\mapsto 
 ℰ ⁡ ( σ t ) \displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\sigma_{t}) 
 
 
 
 
 is injective, where σ t \sigma_{t} is constructed in Theorem 4.1 . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Since X ∈ 𝔹 k − 1 ∩ 𝕊 k + 1 , X\in\mathbb{B}^{k-1}\cap\mathbb{S}^{k+1}, by Theorem 4.1 , we have that σ t \sigma_{t} exists.
If there exists some η t ∈ U k − 1 ​ ( X t ) \eta_{t}\in U^{k-1}(X_{t}) such that ℰ ⁡ ( σ t ) = ∂ ¯ t ​ η t \mathcal{E}(\sigma_{t})=\overline{\partial}_{t}\eta_{t} , then 
 

 
 
 ℰ ⁡ ( σ t ) \displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\sigma_{t}) 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ ¯ t ​ η t \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{t}\eta_{t} 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ ¯ t ​ ( ℍ t ​ η t + Δ ∂ ¯ t ​ G ∂ ¯ t ​ η t ) \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{t}(\mathbb{H}_{t}\eta_{t}+\Delta_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}G_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}\eta_{t}) 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ ¯ t ​ ∂ ¯ t ∗ ​ ∂ ¯ t ​ G ∂ ¯ t ​ η t \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{t}\overline{\partial}_{t}^{\ast}\overline{\partial}_{t}G_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}\eta_{t} 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ ¯ t ​ G ∂ ¯ t ​ ∂ ¯ t ∗ ​ ∂ ¯ t ​ η t \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{t}G_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{t}^{\ast}\overline{\partial}_{t}\eta_{t} 
 
 
 
 
 = \displaystyle= 
 ∂ ¯ t ​ G ∂ ¯ t ​ ∂ ¯ t ∗ ​ ( ℰ ⁡ ( σ t ) ) . \displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{t}G_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}\overline{\partial}_{t}^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}(\sigma_{t})). 
 
 
 
 
 Also, by the assumption that h ∂ ¯ t k − 1 ​ ( X t ) h^{k-1}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) is independent of t t , we have G ∂ ¯ t G_{\overline{\partial}_{t}} 
is smooth on t t  (Theorem 7.6 on Page 344 in [ 12 ] ). So we can let t → 0 t\to 0 on both sides of the formula above and get that 
 

 
 
 σ 00 = ∂ ¯ ​ G ∂ ¯ ​ ∂ ¯ ∗ ​ ( σ 00 ) , \displaystyle\sigma_{00}=\overline{\partial}G_{\overline{\partial}}\overline{\partial}^{\ast}(\sigma_{00}), 
 
 
 that is, σ 00 \sigma_{00} is also ∂ ¯ \overline{\partial} -exact.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Proposition 5.2 . 
 
 Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold. If we assume that X ∈ 𝕊 − n + 1 X\in\mathbb{S}^{-n+1} , then h ∂ ¯ t − n ​ ( X t ) h^{-n}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) is independent of t t , where n = d ​ i ​ m ℂ ​ X n=dim_{\mathbb{C}}X . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 For any σ 00 ∈ H ∂ ¯ − n ​ ( X ) \sigma_{00}\in H_{\overline{\partial}}^{-n}(X) , we have that d ​ σ 00 = ∂ σ 00 = 0 . d\sigma_{00}=\partial\sigma_{00}=0. 
By the assumption that X ∈ 𝕊 − n + 1 X\in\mathbb{S}^{-n+1} and ∂ ( σ ) = 0 \partial(\sigma)=0 for any σ ∈ U − n ​ ( X ) , \sigma\in U^{-n}(X), we have that Equation 4.4 has the solution σ ~ p ​ q \tilde{\sigma}_{pq} and thus σ ~ t \tilde{\sigma}_{t} exists. 
 
 
 If ℰ ⁡ ( σ ~ t ) = 0 ∈ H ∂ ¯ t − n ​ ( X t ) \mathcal{E}(\tilde{\sigma}_{t})=0\in H_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{-n}(X_{t}) , we have that there exists some η t ∈ U − n − 1 ​ ( X t ) \eta_{t}\in U^{-n-1}(X_{t}) , such that ℰ ⁡ ( σ ~ t ) = ∂ ¯ t ​ η t \mathcal{E}(\tilde{\sigma}_{t})=\overline{\partial}_{t}\eta_{t} . Since U − n − 1 ​ ( X t ) = 0 U^{-n-1}(X_{t})=0 , we have that ℰ ⁡ ( σ ~ t ) = 0 \mathcal{E}(\tilde{\sigma}_{t})=0 . By Proposition 3.3 , ℰ \mathcal{E} is an isomorphism and thus σ t = 0 . \sigma_{t}=0. Then we get that σ 00 = 0 \sigma_{00}=0 and Map 5.1 is injective. 
 
 
 And since we have already known that h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) is upper-semicontinuous in t t (Theorem 7.3 on Page 326 in [ 12 ] and Δ ∂ ¯ \Delta_{\overline{\partial}} is strongly elliptic), we prove the proposition.
∎ 
 
 
 
 Thus, we can get the following: 
 
 
 Corollary 5.3 . 
 
 Let ( X , G ) (X,G) be a compact generalized Hermitian manifold which satisfies ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma, then h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) h_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) is independent of t t , where − n ≤ k ≤ n -n\leq k\leq n . 
 
 
 
 Proof. 
 
 Since X X satisfies ∂ ∂ ¯ \partial\overline{\partial} -lemma, by Lemma 2.25 , we have that X ∈ 𝔹 k X\in\mathbb{B}^{k} for any − n ≤ k ≤ n -n\leq k\leq n .
We prove h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) h_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) is independent of t t by induction on t t .
By Proposition 5.2 , we know that h ∂ ¯ t − n ​ ( X t ) h_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{-n}(X_{t}) is independent of t t .
By Theorem 4.1 , we give a map from H ∂ ¯ k ​ ( X ) → H ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) H_{\overline{\partial}}^{k}(X)\to H_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) .
If h ∂ ¯ t k − 1 ​ ( X t ) h_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k-1}(X_{t}) is independent of t t , by Proposition 5.1 , we know that
this map is injective.
Combine the fact that h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) h^{k}_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}(X_{t}) is upper-semicontinuous in t t , we can get the result that h ∂ ¯ t k ​ ( X t ) h_{\overline{\partial}_{t}}^{k}(X_{t}) is independent of t t . 
 
 
 ∎
